- [unverifiable] **Arm Cortex-X4** — Gate-level structure not published: no Arm source discloses Cortex-X4's per-primitive gate/transistor implementation (L1I/L1D array organisation below set/way geometry, TLB CAM cell form, predictor table and counter circuits, BTB CAM cell form, decoder internals, rename-map cell form and port counts, free-list depth, issue-queue wakeup/select circuitry, ROB storage-cell form and banking, dispatch mux radix, pipeline-register widths, integer register-file cell form and port counts, ALU carry-tree depth and logic-unit mux radix, multiplier array geometry and pipeline depth, divider radix and iteration count, branch comparator width and target-adder carry structure, AGU adder width and port mapping, load/store queue depths and forwarding-comparator counts, D-TLB match internals, vector per-pipe lane split and divide/sqrt iteration counts, L2 per-bank port counts and tag/data RAM latencies, CPU-bridge FIFO depths and synchronizer implementation, prefetcher table size, history depth and prefetch distance). All gate-level detail in the Level-4 decomposition is textbook-standard construction, labelled as such. Sources: Arm Cortex-X4 Core Technical Reference Manual, doc 102484_0003_06_en, Issue 06.


### Fujitsu A64FX

#### Down to the gates: The Core Memory Group — a NUMA node as the unit of design

The CMG's 22 primitives decompose into gates the same way every other entry's do: a FIFO is storage plus pointer counters plus full/empty comparators; an SRAM bank is 6T cells plus a row decoder plus column muxes plus sense amplifiers; a CAM is storage plus XOR match logic on a shared matchline; a multiplier is AND-gate partial products collapsed by full-adder compressor trees. Fujitsu publishes no gate-level structure for A64FX — no netlist, no cell form, no macro organisation — so every gate and transistor structure below is the generic textbook construction of the named primitive, labelled **standard construction**, pointing at the primer's bottom layer (universal NAND/NOR gates, latch and master–slave D flip-flop, 6T SRAM cell, CAM cell, full adder). It is never a claim about Fujitsu's actual implementation. The only A64FX-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3; the figures marked arithmetic are derived from those published geometries.

- **Crossbar (the CMG X-bar)** — At gate level the X-bar is a mux/demux fabric plus a switch arbiter. Each data bit travelling from one of thirteen cores to the L2 passes a 13:1 select — a tree of twelve 2:1 muxes, four levels deep by arithmetic (2^4 = 16 ≥ 13) — each 2:1 mux two transmission gates (one NMOS + one PMOS per leg, 2 transistors per input leg) or an equivalent NAND-gate cell, with the select lines decoded from the arbiter's grants. The aggregate 512 bytes/cycle per CMG is 4,096 data bits, so the fabric is that per-bit mux tree replicated across the bus width, in both directions (the return path is the same construction at 256 bytes/cycle). The arbiter is a priority encoder — an AND/OR prefix tree computing the highest-priority requesting core — plus a small FSM (state bits in D flip-flops, ≈20–28 transistors each, primer) holding the round-robin or age state; the arbitration policy itself is not published, so the encoder's priority order is standard construction, not Fujitsu's policy. The mux radix and the arbiter's internal structure are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank (the L2 data array)** — 6T cells in rows: 8 MiB is 67,108,864 data bits, so the data array alone is that many 6T cells — 402,653,184 transistors of cell storage by arithmetic — before decoders, muxes and sense amplifiers. A row decoder — an AND/NAND tree from the 11-bit hashed set index — raises one wordline per set across 2,048 sets; the 256-byte line reads out through bitlines into sense amplifiers, with the two banks (4 MiB each, interleaved at bit[8]) each carrying its own decoder and sense-amplifier row. The published index hash itself is gates: the formula XORs five 3-bit address fields and then XORs the result with the top 3 bits of PA<18:8>, so the hash is an XOR tree — four XOR2 gates per bit for the five-way XOR of the high 3 bits, plus three XOR2 gates for the final XOR with PA<18:16> (the low 8 index bits are wires straight from PA<15:8>), 15 XOR2 gates in total by arithmetic from the published formula. Bank port counts, the tag/data RAM latencies and the bank-internal row/column organisation are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank + comparator (the L2 tag array)** — One tag SRAM beside the data array, organised the same way — 6T cells, row decoder, sense amplifiers — with one comparator per way: sixteen comparators per lookup by arithmetic from the 16-way geometry, each comparator an XNOR gate per tag bit feeding an AND tree that yields a per-way hit, and a 16:1 way-select mux (a tree of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes) steering the hit way's data out. The 37–47-cycle hit latency is pipeline and wire delay across the CMG, not gate depth — the compare itself evaluates in one cycle. The tag's stored width is not stated separately from the published index formula, and the tag array's ECC/duplicate protection bits are not itemised, so no tag-transistor total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the L2 move-in buffer, MIB)** — Storage plus write and read pointer counters plus full/empty comparators: a 256-entry circular queue needs 8-bit pointers by arithmetic (2^8 = 256), each counter a D-flip-flop bank with a full-adder incrementer, and full/empty is an XNOR-per-bit equality compare between the pointers feeding an AND tree, with one extra wrap bit distinguishing full from empty — the standard construction. The entry width is not published, so the storage is described as a flop or small-SRAM array of unpublished width; the address-match comparators used to merge duplicate misses and the MIB's allocation policy are not published. Standard construction.
- **Register file + comparator (the store lock register)** — 244 lock bits held in a flop/latch register file — one D flip-flop per lock bit, ≈20–28 transistors each in a standard-cell library — with a write decoder (an AND/NAND tree from the line index) setting a bit when a move-out starts and clearing it when the writeback lands. Checking a line against the register is a comparator: the incoming line address decoded to its lock entry and the stored bit read out, gated by an AND into the consistency check that stalls a conflicting read. 244 entries need an 8-bit index by arithmetic (2^8 = 256 ≥ 244). The register's cell form and the comparator's exact wiring are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the MAC scheduler queue)** — The same circular-queue construction as the MIB at the same depth: 244 entries, 8-bit head and tail pointers by arithmetic (2^8 = 256 ≥ 244), D-flip-flop pointer counters with full-adder incrementers, XNOR/AND full/empty comparators. Around the queue sits the scheduler itself — an FSM (state bits in D flip-flops, next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network) that picks the next access to issue under HBM2 timing rules. The scheduling policy — row-hit-first, read/write grouping, refresh handling — is not published, so the FSM's state table is standard construction, not Fujitsu's policy. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank + mux (the HBM2 interface)** — The interface's gate-level content is a 1,024-bit-wide data path: a 1,024-bit register of D flip-flops staging each burst, read/write data muxes selecting between the read and write halves of the path (the published 256 GB/s read vs 128 GB/s write peaks mean the write side is half the read side's width by construction), and a serializer/deserializer pair — shift registers of D flip-flops clocked by the PHY — turning the wide parallel word into the 2.0 Gbps per-pin serial stream. 1,024 bits × 2.0 Gbps is 256 GB/s by arithmetic, matching the published peak. The HBM2 PHY's internal structure, the bank/channel mapping inside the stack and the refresh scheduler are not published, so no PHY gate detail is claimed. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue + credit counter (the ring interface)** — The ring router's buffering is a FIFO of the standard form — storage plus pointer counters plus full/empty comparators — sized to the 64 bytes/cycle (512-bit) ring width; its depth is not published. Flow control is a credit counter: an up/down counter — a D-flip-flop register with a full-adder increment/decrement path — decremented when a flit is sent, incremented when a credit returns, with a zero-detect NOR tree (all-zero = no credits, stop sending) and a full-detect compare. Two 512-bit directions (the two-way ring) mean two such counter/FIFO pairs. The ring router's buffering, its flow-control structure and the ring arbitration policy are not published beyond the 64 bytes/cycle rate. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank / CAM (the on-chip directory)** — No gate-level decomposition is claimable here, because the manual and the deck publish no directory geometry at all — not its location, entry count, or whether it is SRAM-indexed or CAM-based. The two candidate forms are both standard construction and neither is claimed: an SRAM-indexed directory would be a 6T cell array with a row decoder and sense amplifiers, one entry per tracked line, addressed by a hash of the line address; a CAM-based directory would be CAM cells (6T storage + ≈4T XOR compare per bit, primer) with one matchline per entry, the incoming line address compared against all entries in parallel and a priority encoder selecting the hit. Which form Fujitsu built, and at what size, is not published. Standard construction — conditional, not a claim.
- **SRAM bank (the L1 data cache)** — 6T cells in rows: 64 KiB is 524,288 data bits, so the data array alone is 3,145,728 cell transistors by arithmetic. The set index is 6 bits by arithmetic (64 sets from the published index formula index_A = (A mod 16,384) / 256), so the row decoder is a 6-bit AND/NAND tree raising one of 64 wordlines; the 256-byte line reads out through bitlines into sense amplifiers. Two 64-byte/cycle read ports mean the array is either duplicated or built from dual-ported cells — each port with its own decoder and sense-amplifier row — but the manual does not say which, so neither is claimed; the two-ports-per-cycle behaviour is published, the cell form is not. The write-port width and banking beyond the two read ports are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank + comparator (the L1D tag array)** — Four tag comparators per access by arithmetic from the 4-way geometry: each an XNOR-per-bit compare against the incoming tag feeding an AND tree, the four hit signals feeding a 4:1 way-select mux (two levels of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes). Synonym prevention at bits[13:12] is a 2-bit address compare — two XNOR gates plus an AND — whose result steers the index through a mux so a 4 KiB page's aliases land in the same set; the circuit's exact form is not published, so the 2-bit compare is the standard construction of the published behaviour, not Fujitsu's circuit. The tag width is not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the L1D move-in/move-out buffers, and the L1I move-in buffer)** — Three small circular queues of the standard form. The 12-entry L1D MIB needs 4-bit pointers by arithmetic (2^4 = 16 ≥ 12); the 4-entry L1D MOB needs 2-bit pointers plus a full/empty flag; the 3-entry L1I MIB needs 2-bit pointers (2^2 = 4 ≥ 3). Each is entry storage in D flip-flops plus pointer counters (D flip-flops with full-adder incrementers) plus XNOR/AND full/empty comparators. Move-in bypass — a response going to the higher cache level without waiting for the move-in to complete — is a bypass mux around the MIB: a 2:1 transmission-gate mux per data bit, steered by the bypass-eligibility signal. Entry widths and the bypass-eligibility logic beyond the published instruction list are not published. Standard construction.
- **Comparator + mux (the combined-gather unit)** — A 128-byte aligned block is 7 bits of block offset by arithmetic (2^7 = 128), so two gathered elements fall in the same block when their addresses agree above bit 6 — a comparator of XNOR gates over the address bits above bit 6, ANDed into a same-block signal. That signal steers a data mux that packs both elements into the vector register in one pass: per result bit, a 2:1 transmission-gate mux selecting between the first and second element's data, driven by the same-block signal and the element-offset bits. The number of parallel gather lanes, the gather queue depth and the scatter-side counterpart are not published. Standard construction.
- **CAM (the L1 TLBs and the translation table cache)** — A fully associative TLB is the primer's CAM cell per stored tag bit — 6T storage plus ≈4T XOR compare — with one precharged matchline per entry: sixteen matchlines per TLB by arithmetic from the 16 entries, each matchline a wired-NOR of its row's per-bit XNOR results, a single mismatching bit pulling the line down. A hit selects through a priority encoder (an AND/OR prefix tree, 16 inputs) that drives the matching entry's physical page number out. FIFO replacement is a 4-bit pointer counter by arithmetic (2^4 = 16), a D-flip-flop bank with a full-adder incrementer advancing to the next victim. The translation table cache is the same construction with LRU replacement instead: per-entry age state (a small counter per entry, or a tree-PLRU — the standard forms, neither claimed) updated on each access. The CAM cell form and the FIFO pointer implementation are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank (the L2 TLBs)** — A 6T cell array with an 8-bit set-index row decoder by arithmetic (256 sets from 1,024 entries / 4 ways), plus four tag comparators per lookup by arithmetic — one XNOR-per-bit compare per way feeding an AND tree — and a 4:1 way-select mux. LRU replacement is per-set state bits: two bits per set for true 4-way LRU (a 2-bit counter per set, D flip-flops with increment logic), or a 3-bit tree-PLRU — the standard forms; the manual publishes the LRU policy, not the state-bit implementation, so neither form is claimed. The table-walk path is not published beyond the table. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue + CAM (the reservation stations)** — Each station is a queue with CAM wakeup. Storage is a flop array: RSA 10 entries × 2 (20 entries), RSE 20 entries × 2 (40 entries), RSBR 19 entries — 79 entries in total by arithmetic — each entry holding an op, its source physical-register tags and a ready bit. Wakeup is a CAM-style parallel tag compare: every entry's source tags are XNOR-compared against the broadcast result tags each cycle — one XNOR gate per tag bit, ANDed into a per-operand match, and the matches ANDed into the entry's ready bit. Tag widths are arithmetic from the published register files: 7 bits for GPR and FPR tags (2^7 = 128 ≥ 96 and ≥ 128), 6 bits for predicate tags (2^6 = 64 ≥ 48). Select is a priority encoder per pipe — an AND/OR prefix tree picking the oldest ready entry — latching its grant into D flip-flops for the issue cycle. The wakeup comparator count per entry, the number of result buses and the issue arbitration policy are not published. Standard construction.
- **Register file (the physical register files)** — Flop/latch storage behind read-port mux trees: every read port costs a full N:1 mux per bit — transmission-gate (2 transistors per leg) or NAND trees — which is why port count, not capacity, prices a register file. The vector file is the extreme case: 128 entries × 512 bits is 8 KiB of storage per core by arithmetic (128 × 512 bits = 65,536 bits), and each 512-bit read port is a 128:1 mux per bit — seven levels of 2:1 muxes — replicated 512 times per port. The write side is a decoder (an AND/NAND tree from the destination index) enabling one entry's write. Physical-register indices are 7 bits for GPR/FPR by arithmetic (2^7 = 128 ≥ 96 and ≥ 128) and 6 bits for PPR (2^6 = 64 ≥ 48). The port counts and the cell form (SRAM-style vs flop/latch) are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the commit stack entry, CSE)** — A 128-entry circular queue of the standard form: entry storage in D flip-flops, 7-bit head and tail pointer counters by arithmetic (2^7 = 128), each a D-flip-flop bank with a full-adder incrementer, and full/empty comparators — XNOR gates per pointer bit feeding an AND tree, plus a wrap bit. The Group ID field is 5 bits by arithmetic (2^5 = 32 GIDs), and up to four µOPs per GID means the commit-head ready check is a small counter per GID (a 2-bit count, D flip-flops with increment logic) plus a comparator against the allocated count. The storage-cell form, the commit-head ready-check circuit and the flush/recovery wiring are not published. Standard construction.
- **Counter (the fetch and store ports)** — Up/down counters of the standard form: a D-flip-flop register with a full-adder increment/decrement path, a zero-detect NOR tree and a full-detect compare. Widths are arithmetic from the published counts: the virtual fetch port (160 entries) and virtual store port (192 entries) need 8-bit counters (2^8 = 256 ≥ 160 and ≥ 192); the real fetch port (40) needs 6 bits (2^6 = 64 ≥ 40); the real store port (24) needs 5 bits (2^5 = 32 ≥ 24); the 8-entry write buffer needs 3-bit pointers (2^3 = 8) plus a full/empty flag. The virtual-to-real promotion policy and the store-port ordering comparators are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the prefetch queue, PFQ)** — A 16-entry circular queue of the standard form: entry storage in D flip-flops — each entry a predicted address plus a prefetch-distance field plus one Strong/Weak flag bit (a single D flip-flop) — with 4-bit head and tail pointers by arithmetic (2^4 = 16), full-adder incrementers and XNOR/AND full/empty comparators. Around the queue sits the address-prediction controller — an FSM (state bits in D flip-flops, next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network) that issues Strong prefetches when resources allow and drops Weak ones when they are short; the drop is a mux steering the Weak entry out of the issue path. The stream-detection table's geometry beyond the 16-entry PFQ is not published. Standard construction.
- **Register file (the sector-cache partition control)** — Partition control is a small register file of system registers — D flip-flops, one per control bit, written through a decoder — plus allocation comparators that steer each fill to its sector: each fill's sector tag XNOR-compared against the partition-control bits, the match feeding a mux that routes the line into the selected sector's way range. The number of sectors, the control-register widths and the sector-capacity adjustment steps are not published beyond the manual's worked examples, so no register count or comparator total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Multiplier array + adder tree (the two 512-bit SVE FMA pipes, FLA/FLB)** — Each 512-bit pipe is eight double-precision lanes, and each lane is a gate-level FP multiplier plus an FP adder. The multiplier: a sign XOR, an exponent adder (a small carry-lookahead adder of full-adder cells), and a 53×53-bit mantissa multiplier — AND gates forming the partial products (or Booth-encoded muxes selecting 0/±1/±2 multiples), a Wallace/Dadda compressor tree of full-adder cells (≈28-transistor mirror cells, primer) collapsing the partial products in logarithmic depth, and one final carry-propagate adder. The adder: exponent compare and subtract, a mantissa alignment shifter (a barrel shifter — a mux tree), a mantissa adder tree of full-adder cells grouped by a carry-lookahead prefix network, then a normalisation shifter and rounding. Pipeline registers — D-flip-flop banks, ≈20–28 transistors per bit — stand between the stages. Sixteen DP lanes per core (two pipes × eight) across 48 compute cores at 1.8 GHz is 48 × 16 × 2 FLOPs × 1.8 GHz = 2.76 TFLOPS by arithmetic, matching the published ">2.7 TFLOPS" peak. The per-lane multiplier geometry, the adder-tree depth and the FMA pipeline latency are not published beyond the manual's per-instruction latency table. Standard construction.

Honesty: Fujitsu publishes no gate-level structure for A64FX: the Microarchitecture Manual v1.4, the Hot Chips 30 (2018) deck and the 2021 Datasheet describe blocks, queue depths, bus widths and latencies, and nothing below that. Every gate and transistor structure above is standard construction from the primer's bottom layer (NAND/NOR gates, D flip-flops, 6T SRAM cells, CAM cells, full adders) — the generic textbook form of each primitive, never a claim about A64FX's actual implementation. The only A64FX-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3. Arithmetic figures are derived from the published geometries (13:1 crossbar select = twelve 2:1 muxes, four levels; L2 data array 67,108,864 bits = 402,653,184 cell transistors; index-hash XOR tree 15 XOR2 gates from the published formula; 16 L2 tag comparators and 4 L1D tag comparators per lookup; 8-bit MIB/MAC-scheduler pointers from 256/244 entries; 1,024-bit HBM2 path at 2.0 Gbps = 256 GB/s; 2-bit synonym compare at bits[13:12]; 4-bit TLB FIFO pointer from 16 entries; 7-bit GPR/FPR tag widths and 6-bit predicate tag width; 79 total reservation-station entries; 8 KiB vector register file; 7-bit CSE pointers and 5-bit GID field; 8/6/5/3-bit fetch/store-port counters; 4-bit PFQ pointers; 2.76 TFLOPS from 16 DP lanes × 48 cores at 1.8 GHz); no per-primitive gate or transistor counts are published, and this decomposition invents none. The on-chip directory is the one primitive with no claimable gate-level form at all — its geometry is unpublished, so only the two candidate standard forms are named, conditionally. (New Level-4 fact record: unverifiable.)

**New fact records added in this run** (appended to `files/fact-records.json`; statuses per the fact standard):

- [unverifiable] **Fujitsu A64FX** — Gate-level structure not published: no Fujitsu source discloses A64FX's per-primitive gate/transistor implementation (crossbar mux radix and arbiter circuitry, L2 bank-internal organisation and tag ECC, MIB/MOB entry widths and merge comparators, store-lock-register cell form, MAC scheduler state machine, HBM2 PHY internals, ring router buffering and flow-control structure, on-chip directory geometry — location, entry count, SRAM-indexed vs CAM-based, lookup latency — L1D/L1I banking and cell form, L1D synonym-prevention circuit form, gather lane count and queue depth, TLB CAM cell form and replacement-pointer implementation, reservation-station wakeup comparator counts and result-bus counts, register-file port counts and cell form, CSE storage-cell form and commit-head circuit, fetch/store-port counter widths and promotion policy, PFQ table geometry, sector-cache sector count and register widths, FMA lane multiplier geometry, adder-tree depth and pipeline latency). All gate-level detail in the Level-4 decomposition is textbook-standard construction, labelled as such. Sources: Fujitsu A64FX Microarchitecture Manual v1.4 (31 March 2021); Fujitsu, Hot Chips 30 (2018) deck; Fujitsu A64FX Datasheet (2021).

### IBM POWER10

#### Down to the gates: The eight-slice engine and the Matrix Math Engine — queues, register files and a 4×2 grid of multiply-add tiles

The two signature blocks decompose into gates the same way every other entry's do: a FIFO is storage plus pointer counters plus full/empty comparators; an SRAM bank is 6T cells plus a row decoder plus column muxes plus sense amplifiers; a CAM is storage plus XOR match logic on a shared matchline; a multiplier is AND-gate partial products collapsed by full-adder compressor trees. IBM publishes no gate-level structure for POWER10 — no netlist, no cell form, no macro organisation — so every gate and transistor structure below is the generic textbook construction of the named primitive, labelled **standard construction**, pointing at the primer's bottom layer (universal NAND/NOR gates, latch and master–slave D flip-flop, 6T SRAM cell, CAM cell, full adder). It is never a claim about IBM's actual implementation. The MMA paper (Moreira et al., arXiv:2104.03142, §III) publishes the MME's physical block structure in unusual detail — the 4×2 grid, the per-half port counts, the transfer cycle counts — which is why the MME half of this section is anchored to verified structure; the gate forms inside those blocks are still standard construction. The only POWER10-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3; the figures marked arithmetic are derived from those published geometries.

- **FIFO / queue (the 512-entry shared instruction table)** — Storage plus write and read pointer counters plus full/empty comparators. 512 entries need 9-bit pointers by arithmetic (2^9 = 512), each counter a D-flip-flop bank with a full-adder incrementer; full/empty is an XNOR-per-bit equality compare between the pointers feeding an AND tree, with one extra wrap bit distinguishing full from empty. Wakeup — the standard construction of a wakeup table — is a CAM-style parallel tag compare: every entry's source tags XNOR-compared against the broadcast result tags each cycle, one XNOR gate per tag bit ANDed into a per-operand match, the matches ANDed into the entry's ready bit; select is a priority encoder per slice (an AND/OR prefix tree) picking the oldest ready entry. The tag width, the number of result buses, the wakeup/select implementation and the storage form are not published — the wakeup description is standard construction, not IBM's circuit. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the per-slice in-flight window)** — The same circular-queue construction at 20 entries: 5-bit pointers by arithmetic (2^5 = 32 ≥ 20), D-flip-flop pointer counters with full-adder incrementers, XNOR/AND full/empty comparators. One instruction issues per cycle per slice, so the window's read side is a 20:1 select — a mux tree, about 5 levels of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes — steered by the issue-select encoder. Eight slices × 20 = 160 in-flight instructions per SMT8 core by arithmetic. The storage form (register array vs SRAM) and the ready-check circuit are not published. Standard construction.
- **Register file (the per-slice vector-scalar register file, VS RF)** — Flop/latch storage behind read-port mux trees: 64 VSRs × 128 bits = 8,192 bits (1 KiB) of register storage per slice by arithmetic. Every read port costs a full 64:1 mux per bit — six levels of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes (2 transistors per leg) — replicated 128 times per port; the write side is a decoder (an AND/NAND tree from the 6-bit destination index, 2^6 = 64) enabling one entry's write. The read/write port counts, the bypass network feeding it, and the cell form are not published. Standard construction.
- **Priority encoder / mux (per-slice issue select)** — Each slice issues one instruction per cycle, so the select picks one ready instruction from the 20-entry window: a 20-to-1 priority choice — an AND/OR prefix tree computing the highest-priority ready entry, about 5 encoder levels from log2(20) — whose grant latches into D flip-flops for the issue cycle and steers the window's read mux and the operand-bypass muxes. The issue arbitration policy between threads and the bypass mux radix are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the load queue and the store queue)** — Two circular queues of the standard form: the 128-entry load queue needs 7-bit pointers by arithmetic (2^7 = 128); the 80-entry store queue needs 7-bit pointers (2^7 = 128 ≥ 80). Each is entry storage plus pointer counters (D flip-flops with full-adder incrementers) plus XNOR/AND full/empty comparators. At the primitive level these queues also carry an address-match function: each new access checked against queued entries — a comparator per entry, XNOR per address bit feeding an AND tree — for ordering and store-to-load forwarding; the forwarding itself is a mux steering the stored data onto the load's result path. Entry widths, comparator counts, forwarding policy and drain order are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank (the L1 instruction and data caches)** — 6T cells in rows: 48 KB L1I is 393,216 data bits, so the data array alone is 2,359,296 cell transistors by arithmetic; 32 KB L1D is 262,144 bits, 1,572,864 cell transistors — per SMT4 half, doubled per SMT8 core — before decoders, muxes and sense amplifiers. A row decoder — an AND/NAND tree from the set index — raises one wordline per set; the line reads out through bitlines into sense amplifiers. Line size, associativity, port counts and hit latencies are not published, so no decoder width or sense-amplifier count is claimed. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank (the private L2 cache)** — 6T cells in rows: 2 MB is 16,777,216 data bits, so the data array alone is 100,663,296 cell transistors by arithmetic, before decoders, muxes and sense amplifiers. Same construction as the L1 banks — row decoder, column muxes, sense amplifiers — at larger scale. Associativity, line size, bank count and hit latency are not published, so no decoder width, bank split or sense-amplifier count is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Lookup table (the 4,096-entry TLB)** — 4,096 entries need a 12-bit index by arithmetic (2^12 = 4,096). No gate-level form is claimable beyond that, because the associativity is not published: a fully associative TLB would be the primer's CAM cell per stored tag bit — 6T storage plus ≈4T XOR compare — with one precharged matchline per entry (4,096 matchlines), each a wired-NOR of its row's per-bit XNOR results, a hit selecting through a priority encoder; a set-associative TLB would be a 6T cell array with a row decoder plus one tag comparator per way (XNOR per tag bit feeding an AND tree) and a way-select mux. Both forms are standard construction and neither is claimed. Standard construction — conditional, not a claim.
- **FSM / controller (the Instruction Fetch Unit, IFU)** — The front-end controller is an FSM: state bits in D flip-flops (≈20–28 transistors each, primer), next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network, the fetched instruction latched into pipeline registers (D-flip-flop banks) between fetch, decode and dispatch. The dispatch side writes into the 512-entry table through its write decoder. Decode width, rename-table structure and dispatch bandwidth into the table are not published, so no state count or decode geometry is claimed. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank + comparator (the branch prediction tables)** — Predictor tables are SRAM arrays of saturating counters: each counter bit is a D flip-flop (a 2-bit counter is two flops plus an increment/decrement adder of full-adder cells), the table read out through a row decoder and sense amplifiers, the prediction formed by a comparator/mux selecting the counter's output. The predictor's table sizes, organisation and the geometry behind the doubled accuracy are not published, so no counter count, table width or transistor total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Register file (the accumulator register file, ACC RF)** — The eight 512-bit accumulators are 4,096 bits of architectural state per MME by arithmetic (8 × 512). Physically each PU-half holds a 64-bit slice of the ACC RF with two read and one write ports: each read port is a 2:1 mux per bit (transmission gates) selecting between the two halves' slices — the paper states each half-ALU's input can come from either half, hence the two read ports — and the write side is a decoder enabling one slice's write. The total physical ACC RF storage per MME and the slice-to-accumulator mapping beyond the 64-bit slice are not stated in the paper, so no ACC RF transistor total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Multiplier array + adder tree (the processing-unit arithmetic units)** — Each PU-half ALU is a gate-level FP multiply-add unit: a 53×53-bit mantissa multiplier for double precision — AND gates forming the partial products (or Booth-encoded muxes selecting 0/±1/±2 multiples), a Wallace/Dadda compressor tree of full-adder cells (≈28-transistor mirror cells, primer) collapsing the partial products in logarithmic depth, and one final carry-propagate adder — plus an FP adder: exponent compare and subtract, a mantissa alignment shifter (a barrel shifter — a mux tree), a mantissa adder of full-adder cells grouped by a carry-lookahead prefix network, then a normalisation shifter and rounding. The narrower modes reuse the same tree at reduced width: 2 single-precision FMAs, or 4/8/16 multiply-adds of 16/8/4-bit data. Per MME that is 8 PUs × 2 halves × 1 DP FMA = 16 DP FMAs = 32 FLOPs per cycle by arithmetic; two MMEs per SMT8 core give 64 FP64 FLOPs/cycle, matching the verified figure; INT4 gives 8 × 2 × 16 = 256 MACs = 512 ops per MME, 1,024 per core, matching the verified figure. The FMA pipeline depth and the adder-tree structure inside each half-ALU are not published. Standard construction.
- **Mux / demux + bus (the fetch buses and the result buses)** — A bus is wires plus the muxes at each end: the fetch bus is a 2:1 mux per bit selecting which VSR group feeds the MME (four VSRs = 512 bits in 2 cycles = 256 bits/cycle by arithmetic), and the result bus is a mux steering accumulator data back to the VSRs (512 bits in 4 cycles = 128 bits/cycle). Up to two transfers simultaneously means two such mux/bus pairs, arbitrated — the arbitration circuit is not published. The bus widths and the two-transfer arbitration are not published. Standard construction.
- **FSM / controller (the two rank-k pipelines, MU2 and MU3)** — Each pipeline is an FSM sequencing the rank-k update: state bits in D flip-flops, next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network, the outer-product step latched through pipeline registers between the multiplier array and the accumulator writeback. The two pipelines share the ACC RF, so the controller must arbitrate the shared write ports — the arbitration policy and the ACC RF port-conflict handling are not published, so the arbiter's form is standard construction, not IBM's. Per-pipeline issue arbitration is not published. Standard construction.
- **Power gate (the MME power domain)** — A power gate is a large transistor — in practice an array of sleep transistors in parallel — in series with the MME's supply (header) or ground (footer) rail: on, it passes current with a small IR drop; off, it cuts leakage to near zero. Around it sit isolation cells (AND/NOR gates clamping the MME's outputs while the domain is off) and retention flops (D flip-flops with a shadow latch on the always-on rail) holding the ACC RF state across the power-down. The gating granularity (per MME vs per pipeline) and the wake latency are not published, so no sleep-transistor count or isolation-cell total is claimed. Standard construction.

Honesty: IBM publishes no gate-level structure for POWER10: the IEEE Micro paper, the Hot Chips 32 deck, the MMA paper (arXiv:2104.03142) and the IBM Developer MMA tutorial describe blocks, queue depths, bus transfer counts and latencies, and nothing below that. Every gate and transistor structure above is standard construction from the primer's bottom layer (NAND/NOR gates, D flip-flops, 6T SRAM cells, CAM cells, full adders) — the generic textbook form of each primitive, never a claim about POWER10's actual implementation. The only POWER10-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3. Arithmetic figures are derived from the published geometries (9-bit instruction-table pointers from 512 entries; 5-bit per-slice pointers and ≈5-level issue-select from the 20-entry window; 64:1 VS RF read muxes from 64 VSRs; 7-bit load/store queue pointers from 128/80 entries; L1I 393,216 bits = 2,359,296 cell transistors and L1D 262,144 bits = 1,572,864 per half; L2 16,777,216 bits = 100,663,296 cell transistors; 12-bit TLB index from 4,096 entries; ACC RF 4,096 architectural bits per MME; 16 DP FMAs = 32 FLOPs per MME per cycle, 64 per core; 256 INT4 MACs = 512 ops per MME, 1,024 per core; fetch 256 bits/cycle in, result 128 bits/cycle out); no per-primitive gate or transistor counts are published, and this decomposition invents none. The TLB is the one primitive with no claimable gate-level form at all — its associativity is unpublished, so only the two candidate standard forms are named, conditionally. (New Level-4 fact record: unverifiable.)

**New fact records added in this run** (appended to `files/fact-records.json`; statuses per the fact standard):

- [unverifiable] **IBM POWER10** — Gate-level structure not published: no IBM source discloses POWER10's per-primitive gate/transistor implementation (instruction-table wakeup/select implementation and storage form; per-slice window storage form and ready-check circuit; VS register-file port counts, bypass network and cell form; issue arbitration policy between threads and bypass mux radix; load/store queue entry widths, address-comparator counts, forwarding policy and drain order; L1/L2 line size, associativity, port counts and hit latencies; TLB associativity and lookup latency; IFU decode width, rename-table structure and dispatch bandwidth; branch-predictor table sizes and organisation; ACC RF total physical storage per MME and slice-to-accumulator mapping; FMA pipeline depth and adder-tree structure inside the PU halves; fetch/result bus widths and two-transfer arbitration; MU issue arbitration and ACC RF port-conflict handling; MME power-gating granularity and wake latency). All gate-level detail in the Level-4 decomposition is textbook-standard construction, labelled as such. Sources: Moreira et al., "A matrix math facility for Power ISA(TM) processors", arXiv:2104.03142, §III, §VI; Starke et al., "IBM's POWER10 Processor", IEEE Micro 41(2) (2021).

### SiFive Performance P670

#### Down to the gates: The 4-wide rename/dispatch fabric — 160-entry ROB, four dispatch buffers, nine issue queues

The fabric decomposes into gates the same way every other entry's does: a FIFO is storage plus pointer counters plus full/empty comparators; a register file is flop/latch storage behind read-port mux trees and a write decoder; a CAM is storage plus XOR match logic; an adder is a carry-lookahead prefix tree plus full-adder cells. SiFive publishes no gate-level structure for the P670 — no netlist, no cell form, no macro organisation — so every gate and transistor structure below is the generic textbook construction of the named primitive, labelled **standard construction**, pointing at the primer's bottom layer (universal NAND/NOR gates, latch and master–slave D flip-flop, 6T SRAM cell, CAM cell, full adder). It is never a claim about SiFive's actual implementation. The only P670-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3; the figures marked arithmetic are derived from those published geometries.

- **FIFO / queue (the 160-entry reorder buffer)** — Storage plus pointer counters plus full/empty comparators. 160 entries need 8-bit head and tail pointers by arithmetic (2^7 = 128 < 160 ≤ 2^8 = 256). Each pointer counter is an 8-bit D-flip-flop bank with a full-adder incrementer — 8 D flip-flops at ≈20–28 transistors each plus 8 full adders at ≈28 transistors each, roughly 400 transistors per counter, standard construction. Full/empty is an XNOR-per-bit equality compare between the pointers feeding an AND tree, plus one extra wrap bit distinguishing full from empty. The ROB storage-cell form, its banking and the retire-head ready-check circuit are not published. Standard construction.
- **Register file (the rename map and the rename pools)** — The rename map is a register file indexed by architectural register number: each entry a D-flip-flop bank holding an 8-bit physical index by arithmetic (2^8 = 256 ≥ 208), read and written up to 4-wide per cycle at dispatch — four read ports, each a mux tree per bit selecting among the map's entries, and four write ports, each a decoder (an AND/NAND tree from the architectural index) enabling one entry's write. The pools themselves are the physical register storage: 208 integer + 208 floating-point + 128 vector physical registers. Entry counts beyond the pool sizes, port counts and the free-list form are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the four dispatch buffers)** — Each dispatch buffer is the standard circular-queue construction: entry storage in D-flip-flop banks, plus write and read pointer counters with full-adder incrementers, plus XNOR/AND full/empty comparators, with backpressure as a full flag gating dispatch. Their depths, entry widths and backpressure signalling are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue + CAM (the nine issue queues and their wakeup)** — Each issue-queue entry is a CAM-style tag compare: every entry's source tags XNOR-compared against the broadcast result tags each cycle, one XNOR gate per tag bit ANDed into a per-operand match, the matches ANDed into the entry's ready bit. With 152 entries in total (4×16 + 24 + 2×16 + 2×16) and two source tags per entry, that is 304 parallel tag compares per cycle in the standard construction, by arithmetic. Ready entries feed a priority encoder per queue — an AND/OR prefix tree, about 4–5 levels from log2(16) and log2(24) — picking the oldest ready entry per port. The wakeup comparator counts, result-bus counts and arbitration policy are not published. Standard construction.
- **Priority encoder / mux (issue select)** — The select is a priority encoder: an AND/OR prefix tree over the ready bits — 16 inputs for the integer, floating-point and vector queues (4 levels), 24 inputs for the load/store queue (5 levels) — whose grant latches into D flip-flops for the issue cycle and steers the issue queue's read mux and the operand-bypass muxes. The arbitration policy (oldest-first vs round-robin vs port-priority) is not published. Standard construction.
- **Adder tree (the two integer ALUs)** — Each ALU is a 64-bit adder: a carry-lookahead prefix tree (AND/OR generate-propagate cells) computing carries in parallel across 64 bits — about 6 prefix levels from log2(64) — plus a full-adder cell per bit for the sum, a logic unit as a mux tree selecting AND/OR/XOR/NOT per bit, and a comparator (XNOR per bit feeding an AND tree) for flag-setting operations. MUL under the first ALU is a multiplier array: AND gates forming partial products collapsed by a Wallace/Dadda compressor tree of full-adder cells; DIV is an iterative FSM driving a subtractor (full-adder cells with inverted subtrahend). The adder's carry-tree depth, the logic unit's mux radix and the MUL/DIV pipeline depths are not published. Standard construction.
- **Adder (the two address-generation units)** — Each AGU is a base-plus-offset adder: a 64-bit carry-lookahead adder (prefix tree plus full-adder cells) adding the base register to the sign-extended offset, with the result latched into a pipeline register for the cache access. The adder width beyond the 64-bit address and the AGU-to-cache port mapping are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank (the L1 instruction and data caches)** — 6T cells in rows: each 32 KB array is 262,144 bits, i.e. 1,572,864 6T cell transistors per cache by arithmetic, before decoders, muxes and sense amplifiers. A row decoder — an AND/NAND tree from the set index — raises one wordline per set; the line reads out through bitlines into sense amplifiers. Line size, associativity, port counts and hit latencies are not published, so no decoder width or sense-amplifier count is claimed. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank (the private L2 cache)** — The same construction at larger scale: up to 512 KB is up to 4,194,304 bits, i.e. 25,165,824 6T cell transistors at the maximum configuration by arithmetic, before decoders, muxes and sense amplifiers. Associativity, line size, bank count and hit latency are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank + comparator (the branch predictor's four pieces)** — The predictors' learning memory is SRAM banks of saturating counters: each counter bit a D flip-flop (a 2-bit counter is two flops plus an increment/decrement adder of full-adder cells), read out through a row decoder and sense amplifiers, the prediction formed by a comparator/mux selecting the counter's output against the fetch address. The Return Address Stack is a LIFO: a small register array of return addresses plus a stack-pointer counter (D flip-flops with a full-adder incrementer/decrementer). Table sizes, history widths, counter widths and the RAS depth are not published, so no counter count or transistor total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Mux / demux + bus (the 32-bytes-per-cycle links)** — A 256-bit bus is wires plus the muxes at each end: each link's sender side is a 256-bit-wide mux (transmission gates, 2 transistors per input leg per bit) selecting the outgoing word, and the credit counter is a small up/down counter (D flip-flops with a full-adder incrementer/decrementer) tracking how many transfers the receiver can still accept, with the full flag gating the sender. Credit-counter depths and the arbitration between the three links are not published. Standard construction.
- **FSM / controller + counter (the streaming prefetcher and cache stashing)** — A control FSM: state bits in D flip-flops (≈20–28 transistors each), next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network, address counters as D-flip-flop banks with full-adder incrementers tracking strides, and the past-address table as a small SRAM bank or register array. The prefetcher's table size, stride-detection width and prefetch distance, and the stashing interface's queue depth, are not published. Standard construction.
- **Pipeline register (the 13-stage pipeline)** — Each pipeline register is a bank of D flip-flops — one per bit of the instruction, its renamed operands and its ROB slot — ≈20–28 transistors per flip-flop in the standard master–slave construction, with a mispredict flush clearing them via an AND gate on the enable (or a synchronous reset). The stage boundaries beyond the 13-stage count and the register widths are not published. Standard construction.

#### Down to the gates: The dual 128-bit vector cluster — DLEN = VLEN, two ALUs, DIV and V2S

The vector cluster decomposes into gates the same way: a multiplier array is AND-gate partial products collapsed by full-adder compressor trees; a register file is storage behind read-port mux trees and a write decoder; a barrel shifter is a mux tree; a divider is an FSM driving a subtractor. SiFive publishes no gate-level structure for the P670, so every gate and transistor structure below is the generic textbook construction of the named primitive, labelled **standard construction**, pointing at the primer's bottom layer. It is never a claim about SiFive's actual implementation. The only P670-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3; the figures marked arithmetic are derived from those published geometries.

- **Multiplier array + adder tree (the two 128-bit vector ALUs)** — Each lane's multiplier is AND gates forming partial products collapsed by a Wallace/Dadda compressor tree of full-adder cells (≈28-transistor mirror cells, primer) plus one final carry-propagate adder; the adder tree is a carry-lookahead prefix network per lane width. Per ALU the narrow lanes reuse the wide tree: 16 INT8 lanes, or 8 INT16, 4 INT32/FP32, 2 INT64/FP64. FP lanes add exponent compare/subtract, a mantissa alignment shifter (a barrel shifter — a mux tree), a mantissa adder of full-adder cells, then a normalisation shifter and rounding. The multiplier array's geometry, the adder-tree depth and the FP16/FP32/FP64 pipeline latencies are not published. Standard construction.
- **Register file (the vector physical register file)** — 128 physical vector registers × 128 bits = 16,384 bits (2 KiB) of register storage by arithmetic. In flop/latch form that is 16,384 D-latch bits; in SRAM form, 16,384 6T cells = 98,304 cell transistors by arithmetic. Every read port costs a full 128:1 mux per bit — seven levels of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes — replicated 128 times per port; the write side is a decoder (an AND/NAND tree from the 7-bit physical index, 2^7 = 128) enabling one entry's write. Port counts, the bypass network and the cell form are not published, so no mux or transistor total is claimed beyond the storage arithmetic. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue (the Vector Dispatch Buffer and the two 16-entry issue queues)** — The standard circular-queue construction: entry storage plus 4-bit pointer counters by arithmetic (2^4 = 16) with full-adder incrementers, XNOR/AND full/empty comparators, and CAM-style wakeup tag compares per entry like the scalar queues. The dispatch buffer's depth and the queues' wakeup-comparator counts and arbitration policy are not published. Standard construction.
- **Mux / demux (the V2S unit)** — A lane-select mux: a 16:1 mux per bit for INT8 lanes (four levels of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes), 8:1 for INT16, 4:1 for INT32/FP32, 2:1 for INT64/FP64 — the select lines driven by the element index from the scalar side. Which lane it selects and the unit's latency are not published. Standard construction.
- **FSM / controller + subtractor (the vector DIV unit)** — An iterative divider: an FSM (state bits in D flip-flops, next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network) sequencing a subtractor — a full-adder array with inverted subtrahend and carry-in of 1 — over multiple cycles, one quotient bit per cycle in the radix-2 form, latched into a quotient register of D flip-flops. Its radix, iteration count and latency are not published. Standard construction.
- **Barrel shifter (vector shifts)** — A barrel shifter is a mux tree: for a 128-bit lane group, log2(128) = 7 stages of 2:1 transmission-gate muxes, each stage shifting by 1, 2, 4, … 64 positions under the shift-amount bits, with per-element shift amounts selecting per lane. The shifter's mux radix and whether shifts share the ALU's adder tree or have a dedicated tree are not published. Standard construction.
- **Comparator (vector compares and the mask)** — One comparator per lane: XNOR per bit feeding an AND tree producing a 1-bit per-lane result, the 16/8/4/2 results latched into the mask register — a D-flip-flop bank of 16 bits for INT8, 8 for INT16, 4 for INT32/FP32, 2 for INT64/FP64 by arithmetic from the lane counts. The mask register's storage form and the compare-to-mask write path are not published. Standard construction.
- **FSM / controller (vector-length-agnostic control)** — A small control FSM holding the VL and VTYPE state in D flip-flops, with lane-mask logic — an AND gate per lane gating the lane-enable with the VL compare (a comparator against the element index) — so lanes beyond VL stay idle. The state registers' widths beyond the ISA-defined VLEN of 128 bits and the LMUL register-grouping control are not published. Standard construction.
- **Multiplier array (vector cryptography)** — AES lanes are S-boxes (lookup tables or composite-field NAND/NOR networks) plus MixColumns XOR trees; SHA lanes are adder trees of full-adder cells with rotate/mux logic. Their lane counts, pipeline structure and whether they share the ALU multipliers or have dedicated arrays are not published. Standard construction.

Honesty: SiFive publishes no gate-level structure for the P670: the launch pipeline-overview diagram, the product brief rev 1.0, the P600 Series page and the November 1, 2022 press release describe blocks, queue depths, pool sizes, link widths and latencies, and nothing below that. Every gate and transistor structure above is standard construction from the primer's bottom layer (NAND/NOR gates, D flip-flops, 6T SRAM cells, CAM cells, full adders) — the generic textbook form of each primitive, never a claim about SiFive's actual implementation. The only P670-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3. Arithmetic figures are derived from the published geometries (8-bit ROB pointers from 160 entries; 8-bit physical-register indices from the 208-entry pools; 152 wakeup entries and 304 tag compares; 4–5 encoder levels from the 16/24-entry queues; ≈6 prefix levels in the 64-bit ALUs; L1 262,144 bits = 1,572,864 cell transistors per cache; L2 up to 4,194,304 bits = 25,165,824 cell transistors; vector register file 16,384 bits = 98,304 6T cells; 7-bit vector register-file index from 128 registers; 4-bit issue-queue pointers from 16 entries; mask widths 16/8/4/2 from the lane counts; 7-stage barrel shifter from the 128-bit width); no per-primitive gate or transistor counts are published, and this decomposition invents none. (New Level-4 fact record: unverifiable.)

- [unverifiable] **SiFive Performance P670** — Gate-level structure not published: no SiFive source discloses the P670's per-primitive gate/transistor implementation (ROB storage-cell form, banking and retire-head circuit; rename-map entry counts, port counts and free-list form; dispatch-buffer depths and backpressure signalling; issue-queue wakeup-comparator counts, result-bus counts and issue-arbitration policy; ALU carry-tree depth, logic-unit mux radix and MUL/DIV pipeline depths; AGU adder width and port mapping; L1/L2 line size, associativity, port counts and latencies; predictor table sizes, history widths, counter widths and RAS depth; credit-counter depths and link arbitration; prefetcher table size, stride width, prefetch distance and stashing queue depth; pipeline stage boundaries beyond the 13-stage count; vector multiplier-array geometry, adder-tree depth and FP pipeline latencies; vector register-file port counts, bypass network and cell form; V2S lane selection and latency; vector DIV radix, iteration count and latency; barrel-shifter mux radix and sharing; mask-register storage form; VL/VTYPE state widths beyond the ISA-defined VLEN; vector-crypto datapath structure). All gate-level detail in the Level-4 decomposition is textbook-standard construction, labelled as such. Sources: SiFive, "SiFive Performance P670 detailed pipeline overview" diagram (November 2022, via CNX Software); SiFive Performance P670 Product Brief, rev 1.0 (2025); SiFive, Performance P600 Series product page; SiFive press release, "SiFive's New High-Performance Processors Offer a Significant Upgrade for Wearable and Consumer Products" (November 1, 2022).
### NVIDIA Ampere A100

#### Down to the gates: MIG — the partition enforced in the memory system

The MIG partition decomposes into gates the same way every other entry's does: a register file is storage behind read-port mux trees and a write decoder; an SRAM bank is 6T cells behind a row decoder, column muxes and sense amplifiers; a comparator is XNOR gates feeding an AND tree; a counter is D flip-flops with a full-adder incrementer; an FSM is state bits in D flip-flops with next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network. NVIDIA publishes no gate-level structure for the A100 or for the MIG enforcement hardware — no netlist, no cell form, no macro organisation — so every gate and transistor structure below is the generic textbook construction of the named primitive, labelled **standard construction**, pointing at the primer's bottom layer (universal NAND/NOR gates, latch and master–slave D flip-flop, 6T SRAM cell, CAM cell, full adder). It is never a claim about NVIDIA's actual implementation. The only A100-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3; the figures marked arithmetic are derived from those published geometries.

- **Register file — the partition table.** Seven rows, one per possible instance, each row holding that instance's SM slice, memory slice, L2 slice, memory controllers and engines. The row index is the instance ID — 3 bits by arithmetic (2^3 = 8, enough for 7) — decoded by an AND/NAND tree to enable one row's write, and each field reads out through a mux tree per bit. The storage form is unpublished — whether the table lives in fuses, flops or SRAM — so no cell form is claimed: in flop form each stored bit is a D flip-flop (about 20–28 transistors, primer) and in SRAM form a 6T cell, and the total width beyond the published geometry is not published, so no transistor total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **FSM / controller — the partition manager.** A control FSM: state bits in D flip-flops (about 20–28 transistors each), next-state and output logic as a NAND/NOR network, walking the fixed profile table — only the published profiles may be created, never arbitrary slices. The profile table itself is a small ROM or PLA (an AND-OR plane) holding the seven profiles. Its internal states, sequencing and table size are not published. Standard construction.
- **Crossbar — the partitioned interconnect.** Each crosspoint is a mux: transmission gates (2 transistors per input leg per bit) or an equivalent NAND-gate tree, with the select lines driven by the partition table's port-assignment fields, so a request from one instance can only ever select that instance's ports. Published: the A100 L2's partitioned crossbar delivers 2.3x the L2 read bandwidth of V100. The crossbar radix and per-instance port counts are not published, so no crosspoint count is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Switch arbiter + priority encoder — grant selection inside the partition.** Within an instance, the arbiter is a priority encoder — an AND/OR prefix tree over that instance's request bits, about log2 levels deep — whose grant latches into D flip-flops for the grant cycle and steers the crossbar's read mux. Because the request pool it sees is already partitioned, the encoder's inputs never span an instance boundary. Arbiter policy and encoder depth are not published. Standard construction.
- **SRAM bank — the L2 slices.** Rows of 6T SRAM cells (primer, bottom layer) behind a row decoder — an AND/NAND tree from the set index raising one wordline — transmission-gate column muxes (2 transistors per leg) and latch-type differential sense amplifiers. Each memory slice carries 5 MB of L2: 5 MB is 41,943,040 bits, i.e. 251,658,240 6T cell transistors per slice by arithmetic (about 251.7M), before decoders, muxes and sense amplifiers; the 40 MB total is 335,544,320 bits, i.e. 2,013,265,920 cell transistors (about 2.01B) by arithmetic. Bank count, associativity and line size per slice are not published, so no decoder width or sense-amplifier count is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Comparator — the address-range check.** Every request's instance identity is checked against its slice: the instance-ID match is an XNOR gate per bit of the 3-bit ID (by arithmetic, from 7 instances) feeding an AND tree, and the address-range check is two bound comparators — each a subtractor built from XOR gates and a full-adder carry chain, the final borrow giving less-than — one for the slice's lower bound and one for its upper. Tag width and check placement are not published. Standard construction.
- **Credit counter — per-instance bandwidth accounting.** An up/down counter per instance: a D-flip-flop bank holding the credit balance, a full-adder incrementer/decrementer (about 28-transistor mirror cells, primer) adding or subtracting credits as requests issue and refill, and a terminal-count comparator — XNOR per bit feeding an AND tree — whose empty flag throttles the instance when its share is spent. Counter widths, refill rates and throttle policy are not published, so no bit width or transistor total is claimed. Standard construction.
- **Mux / demux — instance-ID steering.** A demux keyed on the 3-bit instance ID (by arithmetic): a decoder — an AND/NAND tree from the ID bits — enabling one of up to seven output paths, each path a transmission-gate mux (2 transistors per leg per bit) carrying the request to its instance's SMs, banks or controllers. The dispatch-side and memory-side steering are two such demuxes driven by the same ID. Mux radix and steering-table structure are not published. Standard construction.
- **FIFO / queue — per-instance command and copy queues.** The standard circular-queue construction, replicated per instance: entry storage in a D-flip-flop bank or a small SRAM array, plus write and read pointer counters — D flip-flops with full-adder incrementers — plus XNOR/AND full/empty comparators, with the full flag providing backpressure. Because the queues are per-instance by construction, the pointer and comparator logic is replicated per instance rather than shared across tenants. Queue depths are not published. Standard construction.
- **Pipeline register — the instance ID travels with the request.** Each pipeline stage's register is a bank of master–slave D flip-flops — one per bit of the request, its address and its data — and the 3-bit instance ID (by arithmetic, from 7 instances) rides in the same register, so the partition check made at the front end still holds at the DRAM bus. A stall holds the flops via a 2:1 enable mux in front of each bit. Stage count and full register width are not published. Standard construction.
- **Counter — fault attribution.** Per-instance error counters: D-flip-flop banks with full-adder incrementers, one counter per fault class, charged when the fault-attribution logic — a small register file or CAM indexed by the 3-bit instance ID (by arithmetic) mapping a fault event to its instance — names that instance. Counter counts, register widths and the attribution table's form are not published. Standard construction.
- **Synchronizer / reset domain — fault containment.** Two D flip-flops in series in the receiving domain — the first allowed to go metastable, the chain giving it a full clock period to resolve — at each per-instance boundary, and per-instance reset as an AND/NAND gate on the reset tree per domain, so one tenant's crash cannot propagate into a neighbour's domain. Domain boundaries and synchronizer counts are not published. Standard construction.
- **FSM / controller — the compute-instance split.** A second, smaller partition FSM, built the same way as the partition manager: state bits in D flip-flops, next-state logic as a NAND/NOR network, walking a smaller profile table that covers only the SM slices inside one GPU instance — at most the 7 SM slices, a 3-bit slice index by arithmetic — with no memory slices to subdivide. Its states are not published. Standard construction.

Honesty: NVIDIA publishes no gate-level structure for the A100 or for the MIG enforcement hardware: the Ampere Architecture In-Depth blog and the MIG User Guide publish the partition geometry — 8 memory slices of 5 GB, 7 SM slices, 14 SMs per slice, 98 SMs in the full 7g.40gb profile, 1/8 of L2 per slice, one copy engine per smallest instance — and the isolation guarantees, and nothing below that. Every gate and transistor structure above is standard construction from the primer's bottom layer (NAND/NOR gates, D flip-flops, 6T SRAM cells, CAM cells, full adders) — the generic textbook form of each primitive, never a claim about NVIDIA's actual implementation. The only A100-specific facts in this section are the block and primitive parameters already recorded at Levels 2–3. Arithmetic figures are derived from the published geometries (5 MB of L2 per slice = 41,943,040 bits = 251,658,240 6T cell transistors; 40 MB total = 335,544,320 bits = 2,013,265,920 cell transistors; 3-bit instance IDs from 7 instances; 3-bit slice indices from 8 memory slices; 98 SMs = 7 x 14); no per-primitive gate or transistor counts are published, and this decomposition invents none.
